We continue with the agenda, with the second point. Mr. Advocate of Fairy's Opinion, on his own request, to report on the general lines of action of his department
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-04-04-DSPG023.u6	For this you have the word of Mr. Landlord.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-04-04-DSPG023.u7	Mr. President, ladies and gentlemen, good morning. My appearance today is to present the general lines of the Business Council in the present legislation. It ends with the round that over the last few months we have done all government members to advance the goals of each autonomous department; a few months in which we have not been stopped, but on the contrary, we have worked around to approve budgets in less than one month; to eliminate taxes for young people, large families and people with disabilities who decide to live in less populated areas; to cancel the general wage adjustments that we had to apply temporarily due to the economic crisis; we have already resumed, therefore, the full extra payments for all public employees; we encourage collective bargaining by matching the working conditions of 1,800 professionals of the Equality and Wellness Consortium; we continue to reduce the average period of payment to our suppliers; we have completed a year longer the public deficit; and we strengthen self-government, with our own tribunal, to solve contractual resources in Galicia and be the first autonomous community to start the debate on the new model of self-financing by introducing the expert approach. And we were able to do all of this because we have stability, because the Greeks have opted mostly for a stable government; we have continuity in policies and management, which is allowing for the greatest growth in nine years; and we have planning, we don't improvise; we know what we want and how we achieve it. We count on the Strategic Plan 2015-2020 as a key tool for consolidating Galicia's full economic recovery, starting from a new model based on knowledge, environmental sustainability and territorial cohesion, which seeks sustained growth, around 2.5 % of GDP, and lowers the unemployment rate to around 10%. After the first two years of his vigil, we can claim that the Strategic Plan is being useful and serving to meet the objectives. But we are rigorous and we will fully monitor the progress of transversal targets, an assessment of the main indicators of results of strategic targets and spending programs and an operational follow-up with the degree of progress of the different lines of action that contribute to the achievement of those strategic targets. All this so that the Strategic Plan will continue to be the roadmap of the Galician Union until 2020 and correct the targets or action lines that do not show the expected results without justification. Because, ladies and gentlemen, we are faced with legislation with many challenges, but with a great priority objective: to complete the economic recovery of Galician citizens by a new model of growth that is more resistant to future cycle changes. To achieve this we need to deepen on the model of Galicia's own growth that we have achieved in recent years, with healthy and sustained growth. And why do I say complete economic recovery? Because, fortunately, the situation has nothing to do with what was at the beginning of the past legislation, and we've been growing more and more solid for three years. The latest figures on the closure of 2016 reinforce this improvement, for example, the 2016 GDP. The Greek economy grew by 3.4 in 2016, and it was one of the best behaviors in Spain, two tenths better than the average. This is the best growth data since 2007. We are converging with Spain and Europe. We've grown up three consecutive years, with behavior in 2015 better than in 2014, and in 2016 better than in 2015. But also, I'm less unemployed. Galicia closed 2016 with 22,894 unemployed less than in 2015, with a 10% decrease. It's the biggest fall since the start of the crisis. We've had more than three years of consecutive unemployment reductions in annual evolution, and Galicia is at unemployment levels below seven years ago. Recruitment also increased by 7.55 % in 2016 and undefined contracts grew by 9.8%, and full-time recruitment did so by 8.7%. The data we know today is still positive. In March, there's 9.4% less unemployed than a year ago, and in addition to recruitment generally, it's nearly 15% down. But also, less deficit. Galicia met again in 2016 the public deficit target, which fell to 0.54% of GDP, below the limit, because of the 0.7% that we had autonomous communities. Galicia is consolidated as the reference community of budgetary stability again in 2016, with a deficit below the average in 3 tenths. I reiterate once again at Parliamentary level our commitment to meet again in 2017 the deficit target set at 0.6 % of GDP and thus set in our budgets. Ladies and gentlemen, fulfilling the objectives of budgetary stability is one of the foundations of the healthy growth model that we are pushing. Galicia bets on reducing deficit and spending on interest so as to be able to devote more resources to social spending and investment. But also, less debt. Thanks to this control, Galicia is consolidated among the autonomous communities with less debt from Spain. We closed 2016 with a GDP debt ratio of 18.7%, almost the same as in 2015, and 6.2 points below average. In 2016 we reduced debt interest to 38.3 million euros, which amounts to 92 million if we take into account the last two exercises. Galicia is the autonomous community that has increased its debt volume less since 2008, and currently maintains a debt considerably below the average, when in 2008 it was higher. As for the investor effort, Galicia was located in 2016 as the second largest investor community, twice as much as the average. But also, export record. Exports of goods and services increased last year by 5.5%, 1.3 points more than the previous year. In this good evolution it was key to the record of foreign trade, as Galicia first exceeded the 20 billion euro barrier. I'm now going to report the ten main lines of action planned for this legislation. First, we will consolidate the economic recovery of citizens with sound and sustained growth. We've been in full four years with steady and progressive economic growth, and we're intensifying convergence with Spain. Once we've reached international progress over 3%, we want to keep growth steady. Galicia comes out of the crisis with a more modern and better-prepared economy for the future. We've achieved a healthy growth to have a healthy economy that runs away from bubbles and bets on a productive and competitive economy. In three years we were able to recover the lost GDP in the previous six years, and since 2009 we have improved two per capita GDP positions in the set of autonomous communities and raised it to 89.1%. And the most important thing is that recovery is not only of wealth, but also of employment. We created 40,000 full-time jobs in two years. We also reinforce the weight of public spending, which earns 1.4 points in relation to before the crisis, and now represents 16%, which we had in 2008; right now, in 2016, it's 17.4%. Finally, Galicia comes out of the crisis with a more modern, more open economy, with better services, with a primary sector that has a future, with an increasingly competitive industry and strengthened public services. Secondly, as a strategic line, we're going to work to achieve an autonomic financing model that meets the needs of the Greeks. Without a doubt, one of the most important aspects of this legislation will be the revision of the system of autonomous financing. And, modestly, I think we're starting well. We have already agreed with the expert appointed by Galicia to address this review and we approve a motion on the position we must defend. Ladies and gentlemen, I know that the current model is not satisfactory for a number of reasons. The main ones: it's insufficient and little resistant to crises; it doesn't count on a map of services that quantifys the cost of services of its service; it doesn't properly collects exceptional circumstances that our community has, such as the great dispersion and the high aging of its population. So there's an insufficient assessment of the dispersal variable and a weight of the population protected by the aging factor away from reality. That's why we want to introduce more steps from 65 years of age, because it's not the same cost in social services and health services for a 65-year-old, 85-year-old or 95-year-old. In Galicia we have almost one hundred and ten thousand people 85 or more. What is Galicia's aspiration? We want a system that guarantees the financing needs of the public services we provide. And we want to deal with it among all in order to address it in a way would be the need to review the model and frame the priorities and deadlines to address it. The January Conference of Presidents was a good start, because Galicia defended its position and listened to the position of others. Among all of us are the foundations for agreeing on the revision of the model of self-financing based on three principles: actual cost of services, multilaterality and stability. It is essential to finance the actual cost of services. And for that we need to know what it costs them in every community, with their uniqueities and specificities, and in particular health, which in Galicia accounts for more than 40 percent of the budget. It is essential that the resources that are integrated into the system are negotiated multilaterally, with criteria which guarantee the same quality in the provision of services irrespective of the territory in which they are provided. And it's also essential that the new system of self-financing brings stability and a vocation of permanence, so health and dependence must have these separate ones. This review is already under way and, given the transcendence it will have for our autonomous community, we look forward to the support of the other parliamentary groups. Ladies and gentlemen, I reiterate that our strategy will be guided at all times by loyalty and firmness. Third strategic line: we will complete the fiscal consolidation path and meet the objectives of budgetary stability. Budget stability is essential to ensure the sustainability of the welfare state. We want to fulfil once again our commitment to the Tax and Financial Policy Council, thus completing Galicia's fiscal consolidation process. The steady and progressive reduction of the public deficit was very positive for the Greeks, because it allowed us to maintain the solvency of accounts and our financial autonomy. Galicia is the only common regime community that hasn't asked for a euro of the 156 billion that put the state to the end of the month. But if we demand an exclusive mechanism for fulfilling autonomous communities. And that's why the State, on a proposal from Galicia, empowered the Financial Facility Fund in 2015. Why did Galicia join this fund? Because it has no conditionality, because we maintain our financial autonomy, because it's totally optional, because we can go in or out whenever we want, and because it has advantageous conditions that allow us to save millions of euros in debt interest to spend them on productive spending. Galicia is one of the two communities that can use this mechanism; the rest cannot, because it does not meet its goals of deficit and debt. Galicia can choose: we can go to financial markets whenever we want and use the Financial Facility Fund if we benefit. And this has the name: self-government. The balance sheet is very positive: since the launch of the Galicia Finance Facility Fund, it has saved EUR 340 million. Ladies and gentlemen, I reiterate that fulfilling the goals of deficit and debt is positive for Galicia. Galicia has been the least indebted community since 2008. And do you know what that means? Less financial burdens for those who will come after us, i.e. responsibility. In fact, once the debt-GDP ratio has been stabilized, the goal is to reduce it steadily and progressively. Fourth line of action: we will maintain Galicia among the communities with lower fiscal pressure for middle and low incomes. Galicia will continue to be one of the communities in which middle and low incomes will pay less taxes. We will not raise taxes and maintain during this legislation the reduction in inheritance tax, so they do not pay 99% of the Greeks, and the IRPF discount, which benefits 98.5% of the Greeks, and we will complete the zero tax strategy for those who want to live in rural areas and live in rural areas. This decrease had an impact on the first year of application of EUR 41 million. They are resources that have remained in the hands of the Greek households to reactivate domestic consumption and maintain their heritage in full in case of a family member's loss. This year, the impact will be greater, of some 140 million euros, because it will have an effect on the community's collection of the IRPF discount by 2016, and it will also have a full effect on the reduction of succession tax. Ladies and gentlemen, in addition to not raising taxes, we will analyse annually the possibility of further reductions, always depending on the recovery of public income. And to complement these tax reductions we will also maintain the tax benefits approved in the IRPF, in the tax on heritage transmissions and in the tax on successions and donations. In addition, we will maintain the low rates of marriage transmission tax for the acquisition of habitual housing, both of young people and of numerous families and people with disabilities above 65%. Another fundamental task will be to continue to strengthen information to the taxpayer, to facilitate compliance with its obligations, and the fight against tax fraud, to comply with the Tax Control Plan, based on the principles of justice, equality and economic capacity. Over the four years that the Tax Agency of Galicia has been operating, more than 600 million euros have already increased in undeclared debt. In this legislation, we will boost new technologies and encourage specialization. Fifthly, we're going to commit ourselves to our suppliers for an average payment period of about ten days. Nearly 80 percent of Xunta's bills are electronic, a system in which Galicia has been a pioneer in the whole of Spain since 2010. At present, Galicia is the third common regime community that pays its suppliers more easily, and the average payment period in 2016 has fallen in just over 20 days. We're paying better and more assiduously than most communities, but we want to take one more step and get this legislation to reduce the average payment period around ten days, a really ambitious goal. In this process of generalizing electronic billing, after completing its functioning in all public sector entities, the system for educational centres will be universalized. In addition, we will continue the implementation of the electronic bidding system and the electronic procurement system so that in all public procurement procedures the paper will disappear. Sixth strategic line: we will optimize budget management and coordination and statistics. We will advance in the systematization of multi-year budget scenarios, deepen in budgeting and management towards results based on targets set in the Strategic Plan, develop the budget office to optimize the follow-up by most of the main spending policies and move towards zero in budget management, with the computerization of all procedures, in addition to improving the coordination of the different services. Similarly, in the exercise of our powers as financial guardians over local entities, we will strengthen cooperation and coordination with local corporations to improve their financial and budgetary actions. In the tax field we will redouble collaboration with other autonomous and local administrations to make effective management, enabling them in the latter case to carry out their collection management on an executive basis. And in the search for a more efficient and modern administration close to the citizen, we will implement a telematic system for the presentation of assets, deposits and guarantees to the General Deposit Box, and in this way we will strengthen the supervision of both mutuals and corridors and insurance corridors. Ladies and gentlemen, we count on the Polish Statistical Plan 2017-2021, and in this legislation we will strengthen the information, efficiency, transparency and coordination of the Galego Institute of Statistics with other statistical systems. It is planned to expand information on dependence, care for minors, self-employment, social capital and reconciliation between work and work life, as well as to examine often several productive sectors, such as textiles, mining, the agri-food chain and forestry. Seventh line of action: we will deepen our policy of rationalization and zero rents. We have a capital plan in place for 100 percent of the administrative services to develop on their own premises. In this strategy, we aspire to a unique and recognizable office by the Greek Administration, uniting the image of each and every one of the units to make it more recognizable to citizens. And we want to strengthen the policy of zero renters to phase out renters from places and reinvest savings in the maintenance of the property of Junta, in order to optimize it and put it into value. We have already launched concrete actions: in Vigo, running the City of Justice, taking advantage of the facilities of the old hospital; in Pontevedra, recovering the Benito Corbal building to host services from the Junta, the University of Vigo and the social entities of the city; in Santiago de Compostela, with the project of the new San Caetano, to expand the current administrative facilities with the dependencies at the bus station, once it is moved to the future intermodal station; in Ourense, with the position in value of a local, currently unused, for the new headquarters of the Tax Agency of Galicia, which will allow this autonomic heritage to be optimized. And we are planning to continue with the value of this own heritage with performances in other cities of Greece to locate autonomous administrative services. Another goal is to develop a new Heritage Act, which will bring simplification and unity to this subject, in addition to facilitating its knowledge, consultation and application. We will also continue in a process of continuing to improve the quality of transparency in the public sector which complements the processes of administrative restructuring and simplification already under way. Eighth line of action: we will achieve full stability in public employment. Once all general wage adjustments of economic competition have been cancelled that were necessary during the crisis, a priority is to achieve full stability in public employment. Galicia was the autonomous administration that kept its number of troops more stable during the crisis. We did not opt for layoffs as other communities did; in fact, we currently have virtually the same number of troops as in 2008. Furthermore, Galicia is also the community that has the lowest rate of time in all of Spain: 14% compared to the average of 20.5%. So we have 6.5 points less interintently than average communities, and we want to continue to reduce this percentage. If we consider that the central government's goal is to leave the temporality rate at 8%, Galicia already has more than half the way along, and it will be the community that less relative effort will have to do. Ladies and gentlemen, if today in Galicia we have the lowest rate of temporality, it is because the German Government has called on the opposition whenever it has authorized it and making the most of the rest rate. Now we're kicking him. In 2016 we offer 2,644 squares, more than twice as much as in 2015 and the biggest job offer since 2009. And this year we want to offer another one, which will clearly exceed 3,000 squares. In this sense, let me tell you that time, humbly, has given us reason in Galicia, because we are not adventurers but responsible. There were not so many parties who demanded us to make this summons when there was no legal certainty. And now, when it does, where they rule, they don't call for opposition. I therefore believe that in this matter we must have a little rigour, a little seriousness and a little responsibility. The ninth line of action: we will achieve the full equalization of the working conditions of public employees. In addition to the full stability of employment, we aspire to have full equalization in the working conditions of all public employees. We will use the new instruments provided for in the Public Employment Act, approved in the past legislation, to achieve maximum homogeneity in both pay and employment conditions for the service of the self-employed public sector. In the last year we were able to deal with the unions for two new collectives: forest firemen and the Consortium of Equality and Welfare. In particular, we will complete the integration offers in the joint work regime of the Galician Union and will make operational offers for people who are already employed. We will put in place an axil competitive system that will allow professionals to demonstrate their preference and willingness for the performance of characteristic jobs or in targeted locations; and in conjunction with the Administration will offer them the performance of such posts as coverage needs arise. Besides, we've already agreed with the unions the second activity for forest firefighters, so that the older ones of a certain age don't have to turn out fires anymore. And as I have made progress, we have just signed an agreement with the unions so that the more than 1,800 members of the Galician Consortium of Equality and Welfare Services will have a wage equal to that of the rest of the workers in the Union, because in this government we believe in the stability and equalization of public employment. Another aspect in which we will move will be the exemption of fees for selective processes, mainly for long-term unemployed and persons with disabilities. With regard to this latter collective, in this legislation, the first Greek officials selected by means of a specific call for staff with intellectual disabilities will take possession. For the first time, people with intellectual disabilities will have the opportunity to get a job in Galician Union. This Saturday, April 8, almost 500 applicants will compete for the first call. [[Applause]] We are committed to maintaining and enhancing these access routes. Another measure will be the implementation of a temporary and specific employment access list for people with intellectual disabilities. In short, we seek to consolidate a professional, motivated, efficient and service-oriented public service. In this legislation we will develop the Public Employment Act and modernise the statutory regime and the set of rights and obligations of professionals in the service of citizens and public institutions, and we will deepen the policies that prestigious the figure of the public employee, expand the culture of transparency in the exercise of public service, promote participation and open government and complete technological change in working conditions that allow us to care for citizens 24 hours a day on 365 days a year. And, tenth of all, as a strategic line, we will implement the 2015-2020 Strategic Plan in line with the programming of the European Framework 2020. Ladies and gentlemen, as I said at the beginning of my appearance, to maintain healthy and sustained growth, we will execute the 2015-2020 Strategic Plan. The priority is to consolidate the full economic recovery of Galician citizens. In this respect, I insist that we move forward in the systematization of the budgetary and multi-year scenarios in the strategic plan itself, and in budgeting and managing for results and on the basis of the objectives set out in that plan as well. And to make the most of our resources, we will also improve the management of European funds. Galicia has already implemented Feder and ESF funds for the previous period 2007-2013 online with the European Union average and efficiently and efficiently in terms of both financial execution and operational indicators. But we want to keep getting better. Thus, in seeking greater efficiency in managing these resources, and in order to maximize their impact, Galicia has already designed and approved by the managing authority a new management and control system in line with the new demands of the European Union. We will carry out a continuous follow-up and evaluation of the Strategic Plan and Feder and ESF operational programmes. I believe it is a coherent strategy, in line with the programming of European funds and Europe 2020 strategy, as well as the national plan for European Union economic and social reforms and policies. We plan to use new mechanisms, such as financial instruments that allow reusable investments, which multiply the positive effect and access to the largest number of companies. We will also prioritize the innovative use of all European funds which serve as a lever for the marketing and implementation of innovative technologies. Even so, the use of simplified costs is considered to be an opportunity to achieve greater efficiency in management and control, because on the basis of the opinion approved by the Committee of the Regions — and which led Galicia — entitled "Recommendations for Better Expenditure", we seek the maximum effectiveness and greater impact on the use of the resources of the operational programmes of European funds. I'm going to finish. And I wanted to do it with a call to unity and consensus, which would undoubtedly help to achieve the challenges Galicia has to face as a country. Because the achievements that can be achieved in this legislature will not be made by the Government of Greece but by all the Greeks and all the Greeks. And at the beginning of the year we have hopeful data, because we maintain strong economic growth, with an impact on key sectors of our economy, such as industry and services. These are data referred to in January 2017, which indicate that the service sector has grown to over 10%, which is located in Galicia at the head of Spain. Industry business has grown by more than 13%, the biggest increase in the historical series. The entry of orders into the industry has grown above 25%, more than twice as much as the Spanish average. And exports grew by 12.7% after reaching a historical record in 2016. We're getting much better, but no doubt there's a lot left to do. We're on the right track, but since the German Government, we're aware that much remains to be done to improve the lives of our citizens. Because improving the lives of the citizens of Greece will be the goal that will guide our work during this key legislation, in which we will, among all, consolidate the economic recovery of citizens with healthy and sustained growth. We will work to achieve an autonomous financing model that meets the needs of the Greeks, complete the fiscal consolidation path and meet the objectives of budgetary stability. We'll keep Galicia among the autonomous communities with lower fiscal pressure for middle and low income. We'll get an average payment period for our suppliers around ten days and optimize budget and statistical management and coordination. We will sink into the policy of rationalization and zero rentals. We will achieve full stability in public employment, and also the equalization, full equality, of the working conditions of our public employees. And we'll always have the 2015-2020 Strategic Plan as a guide. Thank you very much.

Counsellor, the truth is that, after your intervention, from the parliamentary group I represent, we can only see that you come here to offer us more of the same, more continuity, more conformism and more submission to the central government; strategic lines that we see are absolutely insufficient for our context, insufficient to place Galicia in a good position for future challenges, insufficient to reactivate its economy and build strategic sectors, insufficient to guarantee equality of the Greeks and the Greeks and insufficient to demand a fair relationship with the Spanish State. First of all, let me tell you, Mr. Martínez, that you have, the truth, bad luck with your calendar. Yes, bad luck, don't laugh. Because until a certain point comes here again with enveloped plans and with the story of the recovery which the People's Party has seen because it enters the script, but, of course, just today, which is the day that the State budgets arrive at the State Courts and that we are going to see what you've been predicting for several days in the press, that we are going to see a new aggravation for Galicia, with the complicit silence of your Government, because it must therefore be a little bochoronous. Because, in order not to improvise — that you say they didn't improvise — it seems rather improvised what happened yesterday, when we had the visit of Mrs. Sáenz of Santamaría to promise us an additional story — you call it — to Galicia. Because he didn't say anything at all and he didn't demand the delays that correspond to this country. Because yesterday Mrs. Sáenz of Santamaría came here to sell directly with 247 million that you call "additional." Because in a context in which Mr Montoro has just announced that he does not rule out debt to the autonomous communities that have contracted it through the FLA mechanism, if in the case of Galicia the advantage of not having resorted to it, the advantage of having been the student number one in cutting and austerity policies, will simply be those 247 million — which, as I will explain, are absolutely insufficient — because he will tell me what we have taken in exchange. For us, this is a simple candy to help them figure out that this government does not paint anything at all in negotiations with Madrid, and it also visibles another issue that we have already raised since nationalism on the table, which is that this model of funding does not come at all to manage our competences sufficiently. The resources — we reiterate — that the State's commitment to the financing system is totally insufficient to finance the basic pillars of our society, such as education, education or social services, and whose expenditure needs, in addition, have been increased in a crisis context. What's wrong? That we also look at this issue again at the consequences of the lack of self-government and the lack of the aspiration of this government. Again we are in a frame, in a system that is diametrically opposed to the values of social justice. And while the expenses of the autonomous communities are absolutely corroborated and regulated, the central administration can spend on its free will. So in the current distribution of public resources we see that it is precisely the current central administration that is overrepresented and absorbs more than 50% of them. If this is the supposed solidarity that we have to thank the Spanish State, it certainly does not seem to us to receive it. We understand that this framework and this model limits us, prevents us from managing our resources and destined them to strengthen basic services, in a context where, in addition, we are far from that recovery that you paint so much. Because it's important to remember that, for example, our country's social exclusion rate is rising this past year, and more than 70,000 people are at risk of social exclusion or poverty. In this context of emergency, we have a model of funding that is not enough and a government that, although we have been delaying three years in the debate of this model of funding, has not even sent three minutes of his intervention to talk about it. Galicia has already lost over EUR 9 billion in this financing model since 2009, and you intend that, with Mrs Santamaría's announcement yesterday, we will comply with 247 million more for health and other destinations. It must be that the fashion of this last week is to announce such alms, which is not even the tale that you told us today here that you saved Galicia through the Financial Facility Fund. So we understand that this is not receipt and that these events serve us to put other kinds of questions on the table. First of all, we understand that the political forces of this country should now be focused precisely on that debate in which you have occupied less than three minutes, a debate before what the German Government is showing absolute abandonment, because for me — Mr Valeriano — being the first to begin appointing an expert, being the first to start a debate... If by appointing an expert, automatically, this Parliament has already given all the debates, because we sincerely do not share that reading. We consider this matter to be unheard of, but we do not give up. And since the BNG we want to put on the table again the need for us to give this House a deep debate on this issue, which opens a presentation in this Parliament to achieve an ambitious and country-wide position. Because it turns out to be obvious, with all the eves we have just pointed out, that it is necessary to go to a model change that recognizes greater economic sovereignty and decision-making capacity over our resources. P. de En Marea on the actions to be carried out by the Government of Greece in relation to the distribution of wage income
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-04-04-DSPG023.u81	No amendments were tabled. Mr. Lago has the floor to make the motion.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-04-04-DSPG023.u82	Good morning. I'm not going to say anything new, but inequality is a very serious problem that affects the whole of the most developed economies in the world, but that strikes the Spanish state with special virulence and therefore also us. The two indicators using Eurostat to measure inequality, the 80-20 index and the Gini index show that Spain is among the countries of the European Union which have a higher level of inequality. According to indicator 80-20, which measures the income difference between the richest 20% and the poorest 20%, Spain occupies the fifth position in inequality. I will allow myself here to give the data of the countries with which we share podium: Servia, Romania, Lithuania, Bulgaria and Spain. The Gini index points out that Spain is the state of the European Union where inequality has grown the most since 2007, far above the countries around us. The Spanish economy is the fifth of the European Union. We are among the ten countries per capita income, but we occupy the last places in inequality, and that is something we should be ashamed of. We have a huge problem, because we are already among the most unequal countries, but especially because this is where social inequalities are increasing the most. It happened with the recession, when there was a huge destruction of employment, and it continues to happen now in the high stage because of the increased precariousness of work and low-wage jobs. He said this in the previous intervention, the exit of the recession is being made on the inequality and precariousness of work, without the least self-criticism, without any willingness to rectify the policies of cuts, compulsive austerity, wage devaluation and, above all, of labour reforms that focused on rights. A recent BBVA report has no doubt confirmed that Spain is one of the countries in which inequality and poverty grew the most during the crisis, and says that the reason is that the effects of the crisis were distributed asymmetrically, bringing the highest cost on the middle and low states of income. Plus BBVA explains why. 75% of household income is labour income, and hence the level of employment and wage level are the variables that determine the processes of inequality and poverty. In particular, BBVA says that 80% of the increase in inequality is due to the increase in unemployment, wage dispersal caused by temporary employment, part-time work and false autonomy. This is a fundamental idea to understand what's going on. The first inequality arises from an unfair distribution of income between wages and profits, which is known as the "primary distribution of income", which has suffered a very negative redistribution against the waged population. That can be quantified. Before the recession, wage income was 47% of GDP in Galicia. Now, in 2016, it's 43% of GDP. This means that almost 4 points have been lost, and this explains that despite our approach to the same level of wealth as in 2017, the wages, the salary, is 2,700 million euros below what there was that year. The salary of employees falls because there are fewer jobs -- I'm going to say it again -- there are 100,000 employees less now than before the recession, but also because they're lowering wages as a result of the wage devaluation that has particularly affected those who already had a lower income level. The exit of the recession is exacerbating inequality, because the people who have already paid the highest price in the crisis continue to pay it now also in the high stage of the cycle. Wage inequality has been triggered by the crisis, as reflected in the data of the Galego Institute of Statistics when it analyses EPA's decisions. In the crisis we lost all the employees, but more than others. The loss of purchasing power of 10 percent less cobra multiplies four times as much as the ones they lost. This worst evolution increases wage inequality. If, before the recession, the income of those who made the most money multiplied seven times the income of those who earned the least, now that coefficient is eleven times. Maybe they don't know them, but I'll tell them who those people are. The 70,000 people in Dec 1 have an average salary of 429 euros gross a month. And that figure makes them directly workers and poor workers. But what is worse is that in 2008 the average wage of that decimation was EUR 509 per month, which means that in seven years they suffered a drop in their income of 16% nominal, which, when taking into account the evolution of prices, is 29%. I will say this again, that I believe there is one Member who has not listened to me: there are 70,000 people in Galicia — IGE data, Dec 1 of EPA — who have a monthly salary of EUR 429 gross, in twelve payments. This data has to cause us all a great deal of concern, but especially those who have the responsibility to rule the country, because inequality grows because of a profound deterioration in incomes from those who earn less. For you to understand well, the difference doesn't grow because the ones above cover more, too, but because the ones below get less and less. The analysis of the composition of Dec 1 makes a precise X-ray on the reasons behind poverty and wage inequality. Those 70,000 people work part-time, in sectors of low-value added services, mainly women, in their majority new people, who are employed in small private sector companies and who have a temporary contract, a lot of rotation and therefore short professional careers. The consequence of precariousness and inequality is an increase as unbearable as unacceptable as the so-called poor workers. I've already said again that, since I have little credibility as a union economist, I can only bring right-wing authors. We have a very high precaution and we're not able to generate quality jobs." It is the opinion of the Confederation of Galician Entrepreneurs, not only the opinion of the unions or of En Marea. We are therefore faced with an unfair model, where growth is not recovery, because improvement in macroeconomic variables does not move to a substantial improvement in living and working conditions of the social majority, especially those at the bottom of the social pyramid. The government can't look elsewhere and pretend it's okay. In Galician society it is forming a wide segment of poor workers and workers. Many of them are young people with a high level of education who exceed 30 years without ever knowing what an indefinite employment contract is. It's the people over 45 who have lost their job in the recession and now suffer to go back to work even if it's temporary. They're the ones who work a few months a year at the hostel in the tourist seasons or in the trade in the special sales campaigns. It's the construction workers who now rotate between unemployment and short-term peonage work. It's the 90,000 long-term unemployed who have been unemployed for more than two years. It is creating — and that never happened in the Spanish state or Galicia — a segment of poor population. We're seeing how it grows, how there are more and more people there, and many are -- you know -- people we know -- kids who have to emigrate, people who don't find work, people over 45 who are practically run out of the job market. We can't look somewhere else. And in that sense, we make a proposal, a motion, in which we made an effort to lower the tone, not to pose unacceptable demands for the government party, because we think we have to compromise on this objective. In that sense I synthesized him in 30 seconds. We plan that the agreement with social agents — employers and trade unions — should be sought in the field of the Economic and Social Council and the Galego Labour Relations Council to quantify inequality, on the one hand, to establish strategies, on the other, and to agree on a strategy for the growth of the real wages sustained in time that allows for the recovery of purchasing power. Luis Manuel Álvarez Martínez and Da María de la Concepción Burgo López, on the launching by the Government of Galicia of the mechanisms necessary to give consensus to the political forces the position that Galicia will defend in the General Commission of the Autonomous Communities of the Senate in relation to the Covenant on Social and Political Status for Education
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-04-04-DSPG023.u135	This proposal has been tabled with an amendment by the Parlia­mentary Group on the Environment.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-04-04-DSPG023.u136	Mr. Álvarez Martínez has the floor to make the proposal.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-04-04-DSPG023.u137	Thank you very much, Mr. President. Good afternoon. Despite the commitment of time and the, I understand, of the quorum that exists in the House — although we are slowly incorporating ourselves — I will try to urge the support of the other three parliamentary groups to try to reach an agreement so that the Autonomous Community of Galicia, through the Councillor for Culture, Education and Universitary Order, can defend an evaluated position with as much consensus as possible within the different sensibilities that exist in this autonomous community with regard to education. As we all know, the Spanish educational system, as defined in the LOXSE — the organic law on general management of the education system — and despite the successive changes that have been made through other educational laws, has some essential characteristics which together distinguish it clearly from other European educational systems as well as from other countries. These fundamentally differentiated characteristics are, firstly, a widely decentralized system — in fact, the 17 autonomous communities have the capacity to sort out different essential aspects of the system — with a coordinating body, which is the Sectoral Conference on Education. Secondly, it is — whether we like it or not — more to some, less to others — of a mixed system, i.e. the coexistence of public ownership centres and private ownership. It is a graduated education system, based on the age of students, with a limited number of repetitions per stage. It's a system with a multilevel curriculum model, i.e. an open and flexible curriculum design and a adapted curriculum development model based on center autonomy. It is a system based on a model of comprehensive schooling; that is, all students up to the age of 16 share a common culture in the same type of school, and governed by the principle of inclusiveness; that is, all students — except duly justified and exceptional cases — are enrolled in ordinary schools. And finally, it's a system that empowers educational centers and forces them to manage essential educational processes through their educational projects at the center. The content of these characteristics that I have just mentioned, you can say that our state's educational system is a complex system in which educational agents have a wide margin of freedom to make their decisions and, consequently, a high degree of co-responsibility — not as responsibility — solely in more than one area. Or, in other words, in a mutually conditional relationship between the different decision-making bodies, it is not mechanical, it is based on the ability to communicate between each of them and the determined will of understanding. And this is not a minor issue, because it is the basis on which this non-law proposal is based that we are going to debate in this House today. That is to say, it is not an automatic matter, but it requires a determined will of coordination and loyalty between all the parties involved in the educational process. It's true that the decentralization of educational systems can have some advantages over the adaptation that the educational response gives to the conditions and characteristics of educational institutions and their students, but it's also no less true that it exposes a great difficulty, and that difficulty is to achieve the sufficient consensus so that the education system, despite its differences, functions as an educational system and achieves the goals for which it has been designed. The autonomous communities today, both competently and financially, assume an essential part of public action in the Spanish State. They do so from their own legitimate political options — no one questions it — but with consequences for all citizens. In other words, one thing is the representativeness that exists in the different autonomous chambers of the different sensibilities and political forces and another is that the decisions taken there — and here they are — affect the whole of educational communities. And that's why we have to demand that communities and the state itself be characterized by loyalty and mutual responsibility. Social and equality policies are likely to constitute the scope of public action where the highest level of interrelationship takes place among the three governmental bodies in our country: State administration, autonomous administrations and local administrations. And this characteristic must we understand that it represents a strength of the system itself, because competences come down to the level of government that can most effectively manage them, which has generated — and generated — very important benefits in terms of proximity and quality. For the system to work properly, we need full collaboration between those three levels that it referred to earlier. And the public authorities have — and we have, as far as we are concerned — the obligation to ensure the effective exercise of rights and to do so effectively using public resources to defend democratic values and equal opportunities. And to take forward the set of initiatives that will enable us to build an educational system that will make it possible to successfully overcome the challenges that it exposes to us in the 21st century it is necessary to invest more and more in education. And in that sense, we believe that policies and actions that are carried out in the various legislative developments from the public sector, institutions and governments are fundamental to achieving a model of a fair, solidarity, equality of opportunity and active action in the fight against social exclusion. And in that sense — like all of us here — we know that at the Conference of Presidents of Autonomous Communities that took place in Madrid on January 17 this year, among other things, we agreed to entrust the Government of Spain with the request for the convening of the General Committee of Autonomous Communities in the Senate to hold a monographic debate on education, incorporating that debate — and according to what I said earlier — the diagnosis and the approximation of it to the work of that subcommittee for the Social and Educational Status Pact and for education and thus seeking the maximum coordination among all those involved. What we are proposing today to this House is that the Government of the Autonomous Community activates the appropriate mechanisms to ensure that Galicia's position is defended and understood within that conference. And we are aware that this meeting that took place last month at the Senate on 27th, in which the Minister of Education, Culture and Sport and also attended the advisers and advisers of the various autonomous governments, is only the beginning of that pact and that contribution that must be made from the autonomous communities. Because, frankly, I understand that all of us here would not be able to come to believe that in a three-hour meeting where the Minister of Education, Culture and Sport speaks and speak to the advisers and advisers of the seventeen autonomous communities — he recommends: I am speaking of three hours — if he is able to transfer the different sensibilities that exist with regard to that social and educational pact to which we must be able to arrive between all political forces, social representatives, trade union representatives and civil society in general to define what we consider to be a stable model of our educational system that respects — like! — all the singularities and differences that each of the autonomous communities has, but that it will distort our educational and armoured system from a structural point of view — which is almost the least, but it is very important — and above all financial, our educational system, so as to ensure that the educational system, regardless of whom they govern in the state and in each autonomous community, we have the resources we need. And when I say among all of them, I believe that I am calling all political forces to reach a minimum agreement to guarantee that necessary stability. Because they believe me — and now I make almost a personal assessment — that in the educational system we are full of so much change, so much uncertainty and so much change depending on who governs in the Spanish state and in each of the autonomous communities. And that is why I think it is the responsibility of all of us here to support this initiative, which, I insist, has no other will but to call for a unanimous agreement to be agreed among all of us on what we want to move to that future social and educational pact at the state level. Thank you very much.

It is urgent to put this law down and reverse the privatization, elitist and segregation path of it. The education pact can't be that smoke curtain so we can wake up and continue to apply the most perverse consequences of this law. Secondly, to reach an agreement that budgetaryly blinds expenditure in education. We've already said it in the budget debate: we have to change the cost ceiling perspective through the income floor. That's why we propose to set 7% of GDP as a minimum percentage of investment in education at the end of this legislation. That would indeed be a real 2020 target of European convergence. And thirdly, to articulate the mechanisms necessary for the entire educational community to participate in the debate and drafting of the new law. We don't want a pact and a new law of order, as we've already had many; we don't want pacts and laws that when they apply to reality fail sharply because they've turned their backs on the educational community. We're in agreement with a parliamentary political pact, you have to go further. That is why we are presenting the amendments to the Socialist Party's proposal, in order to be able to make real progress towards a social and citizen alliance that guarantees public, secular, inclusive, comprehensive education that promotes equity and co-education — which now seems to the ladies and gentlemen of the People's Party no longer like — democratic, recognition of the multinational reality of the state and budgeted. To secure those points, on our side, the agreement will be possible. Ladies and gentlemen of the Galician Socialist Party, speak to your colleagues in Madrid and tell them not to continue giving air to the State Government in its educational counter-reform. As Martin Luther King said, murdered today forty-nine years ago, it's always the right time to do the right thing, and this would be the ideal time to put a stop to the neoliberal offensive that counts with teaching to make it effective. Nothing else.

So, and before we finish, we understand that it is not time to limit voices or fix positions, but rather that it should stand for further the opportunity or not to define a unique position of Galicia and this House if the interests of the Autonomous Community were to be attacked. We will follow closely the evolution of this whole process and have no doubt that this group will be up to what the circumstances demand. Nothing else and thank you very much.

ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-04-04-DSPG023.u152	Good afternoon, gentlemen, Mr. President. The chestnut sector, a spring sector in Galicia after a latent abandonment since the 1980s, is again threatened by the possible advancement of Dryocosmus, or, what is the same, the beeve of the chestnut — as is commonly known —. Galicia is Spain's main autonomous community in chestnut production and export. The last campaign exceeded 20 million kilos, which translate into an estimated value that generates about 100 million euros a year. It is now finding, by means of companies, producers, sellers, owners, the widespread concern throughout Galicia for the advance of this plague. Today, the spider is expanding at a high speed, without any measures, such as pruning or cuts of feet affected, and the few that have been done have not succeeded at all. The plague of the pineapple can cause serious economic damage to the production of chestnuts, but especially the forest heritage of centenary bumps throughout the eastern part of Galicia. The hope of the chestnut sector is set in the biological struggle against the plague through the release of its parasite, Torymus. In Galicia, the experimental battle of the chestnuts with Torymus began in 2015, with the release of some 10,000 specimens of the insect in the provinces of Lugo and Ourense, the most present of the problem. This is not only because the bird attacked more the provinces of the interior, but because the rest of the country has a little eucalyptalized, there's little chestnut. The ability of the pest to multiply from year to year is the main concern of the sector, because each female in the bean can lay between 100 and 200 eggs per campaign. The avespa was detected in Galicia in May 2014, not last year 2016, as our advisor stated in the audience of the previous plenary, I don't know whether by ignorance or by misrepresenting that data—I mean, Mr. President, that you lied... or I can tell you—and secondly, because the draft of Galicia's own publication is 2015 and tell us that it will invade much of the Castilian populations of the autonomous community in four or five years. [[Laughter]] According to experience in previously infested countries, the only effective measure to maintain the plague below the damage threshold and thus regaining the productivity of the shipbuilder is biological control through the Torymus sinensis parasitoid. This autochthonous parasite of the Chinese avian had not achieved any high rates that were needed to keep the pest under control. Now, if I may, a brief description, imitating Mr. González, the friend and companion talking about the elbow in the previous commission. Dryocosmus, a hymenopteric insect, such as bees and spiders, native to China, begins its dispersal from 1941 with its dispersion through Japan, in 1974 it arrives in North America and in 2002 it reaches the Italian coast and a few years later it is spread in much of the European chestnut area, specifically in southern France and throughout the Iberian peninsula; the first time in Catalonia, in the Peninsula, in 2012 in Catalonia, and in Galicia and Portugal in 2014, the year in which it officially mentions its presence in eight localities in the provinces of Lugo, La Coruña and Ourense. The Chinese beetle of the chestnut is considered the most important pest of the main species of beetle around the world. It causes losses that can exceed 50% and even reach 100% of chestnut production loss. It weakens the populations and crops of this species, which is so important in our Galician landscape and in the rural economy of many central and eastern Galician towns. The speed of its spread and the enormous impact on the trees makes it necessary to take protective measures, firstly, by the owners of the huts; secondly, by the vivairists and the vivairists, which are threatened by their productions and their plantations; and thirdly — which I believe, not because it is the last, is less important — by the Councillory of the Rural Environment of the board of Galicia. And why? Because it's a plague that's affecting 221,900 acres of bumps in Galicia, chestnut production-oriented bumps that exceed on occasion, as we mentioned earlier, the 20 million kilograms per season. The Chinese beeve of the chestnut comes to the yellow souts at the time of the reactivation of the crop, in which many landowners and landowners and souts see chestnut production as a good productive alternative at a time when a great effort is being made for the establishment of new souts and the restoration of old souts. The presence of the avespa also worries very directly those responsible for the Lourizán Forest Research Centre — so they have expressed it — that model centre to which the People's Party continues to deprive of a decent budget, in which they retain collections and conditions of traditional varieties of more than a hundred different species recognized in the IXP "Galic Castle". The beekeeper's bee is an important threat to all the work developed by the teachers in Lourizán, so protection measures should be put in place. The fact that populations are made up of only females, which lay between a hundred and two hundred eggs each, gives the species the ability to establish or regenerate a population from one individual. It also gives you a great reproductive capacity, which translates into very rapid population growth. Besides, like other invasive exotic species, it benefits Europe from the absence of effective natural enemies that regulate its populations, limiting its dispersion and density. The adults, with a size of between 2 and 2.5 millimeters, emerge from mid-May to late July and live about 10 days. They leave the bug prepared to omit a load of more than a hundred eggs. Posts take place on the new gems developed on the new gems growing. Every gemma can have within it up to twenty eggs. The eggs hatch within the yolks at thirty or forty days, giving birth to larvae, which remain inside them during the fall to winter. The next spring, when the chestnut starts to dock, the hibernate larvae resume activity, induce the formation of the bugs, feed inside the bugs for about twenty-three days and when they occupy the candle, i.e., the chestnut does not produce fruit. There are two ways of scattering the beeve of the chestnut tree: the natural, also called short-distance dispersion, by a direct flight of the insect itself, without man's influence; it is reaching, moving annually between three and four kilometers at least and eight or ten as a half, up to about twenty-five in the most extreme documented cases and the non-natural dispersion, by all known, by means of human transport and by the distribution of infested vegetable material. Official data indicate that in May 2014 there were eight points affected throughout Galicia. Considering a natural dispersion rate of eight kilometers per year and taking into account long-distance dispersion, it foresees that in only four years, that is, next year, most of the inhabitants of the Galician chestnut are completely infected. One of the questions we ask ourselves — and these data that we have just given here are all data from Galician Union itself — if in just three years the pineapple is present throughout the country, what effective measures has the Rural Media Council taken all this time? Performance losses do not occur or are very low-impacted until 30% of the broken gemes are reached or overcome. When you look at four to six bugs that have been slaughtered by a winter branch of about 50 centimeters, you can estimate that the losses will already exceed 50 per cent. The second or third year of the infestation of a jump, the losses reach between 50% and 80%, even reaching 100%. If yield losses are prolonged, a massive abandonment of the crop is most likely to occur. Torymus — biological control through the release of this parasite — is the realization of biological control. The only specific parasite is the only known effective measure to maintain the populations of avespa below the damage threshold. In many countries, this means of control, discarding chemical warfare, its economic costs, its environmental costs and the low effectiveness of chemicals. It's the only effective way to control the bumps. The introduction of an exotic species carries with it risks to biodiversity and the habitats present. In this case, the main ones are the possibility of hybridization with other autochthonous Torymuss that already exist in the Quercus fortress, so not in the pyrenaics. Non-introduction may pose greater risks to biodiversity and habitats by modifying species composition or load capacity. The Ministry of Agriculture, Food and the Environment, in collaboration with the Phytopathological Station of the Area, asked the Ministry of Agriculture, Food and the Environment to import the parasitoid for biological combat tests, both in laboratory and in field. It is a matter of obtaining results from which the Ministry can assess whether it is possible to use the use and marketing of this body as a means of control. This authorization has already arrived in 2015 and today almost no release has been made from Torymus. In a new one that came out four or five days ago in the voice of Galicia, in an interview with the president of the protected geographical indication, Mr. Quintás, he said, "The chestnut sector is asking for massive loose ends to stop the bird. The chestnut light has already spread to 80% of the trees...

ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-04-04-DSPG023.u164	Now I'm reading an institutional statement that made me arrive at the groups on the occasion of the International People's Day: "On 8 April, on the occasion of the International Day of the People of the Shit, the Parliament of Galicia wishes to join in this celebration which recalls the Ith Gypsy Congress held in London on 8 April 1971, in which the flag and the Gypsy anthem were established. In a process of progressing in the necessary and fair institutional recognition of the Gypsy people, these symbols can be used on a protocol basis in commemorations, events and institutional events relating to the Gypsy people, since last March 9 the Congress of Deputies unanimously approved a non-legislative proposal by which "the Congress urges the Government to declare April 8 as the Day of the Gypsy People, recognizing the blue and green flag with a red wheel of sixteen radios and the 'Gelem, Gelem' as the anthem of the Gypsy people." From public authorities we must contribute to this institutional recognition to a community with its own history and culture, which has been based in Spain for more than five centuries and which, however, remains the most socially rejected minority in both Spain and Europe. This International Day of the Gypsy People must be a day of celebration for the Gypsies and the Gypsies, to which we must increasingly join the institutions and the whole of society. It is a day to remember the history of the Gypsy people, their long and painful pilgrimage of expulsions over the centuries; a day to remember the victims of Nazi genocide and so many persecutions throughout their history. This 8th April is an opportunity to recognize the Gypsy community as part of our citizenship, to show the diverse and plural reality of the Gypsy people and to value their contributions to the construction of our society. Despite the progress that has been made in recent decades, the Gypsy community continues to suffer levels of poverty and exclusion that reveal the great rift of inequality that often prevents them from fully exercising their rights. The high rates of school failure among Roma students, the difficulty of Roma people to access the labour market, and the situation of the sub-life of many families seize any opportunity of social advancement. This must be added to the bad social image of the Roma community, based on prejudices and stereotypes deeply rooted in our society, which often stem from deep ignorance and which generate situations of rejection and discrimination. Public authorities have the responsibility to ensure equality for all citizens, including Gypsies, by putting in place the measures necessary to reduce social inequalities, combat discrimination and anti-Citrusism and encourage the active participation of Gypsies and Gypsies in all areas and spaces in our society. On 8 April, International People's Day, it's a good opportunity to visit the Roma communities and celebrate this day together with our Gypsy and Gypsy citizens, and we ask the whole society to join this commemoration." ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-04-04-DSPG023.u167	Thank you very much, Mr. President. The fact is that this Member could not dream of a better preamble than what has just been read to advance the debate on the initiative that we are taking today and that we are bringing here right now. First of all, ladies and gentlemen, allow me to begin this intervention by greeting the people who accompany us today in the guest gallery of the Galician Parliament. On the one hand, I would like to welcome the representatives of the Islamic community of Galicia, led by their president, Salam aleikum! We are also accompanied this afternoon by representatives of the Jewish community of Israel of Galicia, all of them, Shalom! And let me say that I especially greet Mrs. Ania Horszowski, a survivor of the Lwow ghetto, and that 95 years ago she accompanied us with the emotion that caused her to know the presentation of this initiative. I especially appreciate it. About two years ago, and with the gallery of the presentation of a book on religious minorities in Galicia, sponsored by the Pluralism and Convivence Foundation, I had the opportunity to establish a first contact with representatives of different religious confessions of minority implementation in Galicia. During the course of that act, and in the brief conversation that we had later, we agreed that there were no serious problems of coexistence arising from religious intolerance in Galicia, without the increase in recent years of people who process non-Catholic creeds had assumed no significant problem in our country. In addition to finding this reality, the representatives of the various confessions took the opportunity to transfer different claims of a very different nature to the autonomous administration. Among them, he highlighted a very remarkable one: the absence of spaces to be able to bury his loved ones, meeting the requirements set by their respective religious beliefs. In successive meetings we had the opportunity to know more often the problem arising from this circumstance. On the one hand, we are confronted with the problems of citizens of foreign origin, who in many cases opt to subscribe to insurance that allows their remains to be repatriated to their country of origin. If you choose this citizen to remain in Spain, you should be transferred to one of the cemeteries of your confession, which if in many cases exists in other autonomous communities. When the economic possibilities do not allow the subscription of this insurance, it is the other members of the community who must lay down different solutions, in order to be able to repatriate these funeral remains, assuming a very complex administrative tariff, in addition to paying for surely important transfer costs. If the deceased is to be transferred to North Africa, for example, the cost can be around EUR five thousand. If its source is located in farthest latitudes — which is not uncommon — it can reach even the fourteen thousand euros. All these circumstances, as you will understand, contribute to making even more difficult a trance, already in itself painful, as is the loss of a loved one. But not all citizens who profess these religions are foreign citizens. There is a growing number of Greek citizens who, well because of their family context in which they were born, either through conversion processes, with origins sometimes in marriage, or sometimes simply in personal reflection, have no more homeland than our own. These citizens, like many others who are not born in Galicia, are already feeling fully Greek, must choose at their last moment between love for their country and love for their religion. We don't think this is a fair choice. Therefore, after these meetings, we collected the glove that was released to us and included in the electoral program of our party, the People's Party of Galicia, the commitment to promote the creation of cemeteries for minority confessions in Galicia. Today we have come here to take the first step towards fulfilling that commitment, and we have done so from the conviction that we can solve a problem of a human nature and from the conviction that with this solution we are moving forward in the exercise of the civil rights of minorities in Galicia, minorities and the majority as well. Because, ladies and gentlemen, the People's Party of Galicia defends the principle of religious freedom and of the cooperation of public authorities with the confessions, enshrined in Article 16 of the Spanish Constitution, which in its literal content states: "It guarantees the ideological, religious and religious freedom of individuals and communities without further limitation, in their manifestations, that the necessary for the maintenance of public order protected by law. - No one can be forced to declare his ideology, religion or belief." And, finally, "No confession will have any state character. The public authorities will take into account the religious beliefs of Spanish society and maintain the consequent relationships of cooperation with the Catholic Church and other confessions." Ladies and gentlemen, at a time when the attacks on the public projection of any religious element appear to be justified below, in my view, false argument that democratic perfection is achieved only through the application of secularist principles, we want to make a defense of the democratic model established in our Constitution, which strongly bets on granting a public status to the religious fact of any confession and without limit that establishes the only book that all of us represent and everyone is obliged to us: the Spanish Constitution of 1978. So we are not going to assume that the exercise of this religious freedom is intended to restrict itself to the sphere of the private sphere of individuals, in the same way that we could not tolerate that we could only exercise our right to free expression in the privacy of our homes. Today, under the roof of the institution that represents the sovereignty of the Greeks, we find ourselves people of different creeds and opinions. The Galician Parliament, surely the highest expression of public debate in our country, welcomes right now, at this very moment, Catholics, Muslims, Jews, atheists and agnostics, everyone with their belief, everyone with their opinion. I sincerely believe that few times Galicia plural has been as well represented as today. And all this here, in the field of the most public debate in all Galicia. This is Galicia that we like, tolerant, plural and able to collaborate to solve the problems and difficulties of its likes. The approach we make to them today also requires the collaboration of different administrations. The Galician Union is the driving force to address a problem that affects a large number of Galician families, whether they're of origin, or because of their sense of belonging to this land. But we must not forget that cemeteries are a basic competence of the local administration, and consequently this administration would be responsible for the provision of it. And despite the increase in the number of citizens professing minority religions, the municipalities could hardly solve this problem in an individual way, mainly because of the geographical dispersal of this population and the lack of critical mass that justifies the creation of cemeteries of these characteristics in each of the Greek municipalities, or even in one-off municipalities. That is why we believe that the best way to address this issue is through collaboration with the Galician Federation of Municipalities and Provinces, legitimate representative of the whole of the Greek municipality, in order to seek the most appropriate solution to each case in a consistent and as rationally possible manner. At a time when walls and barriers are raised in different parts of the world to protect us from those who think or feel different, today we have the opportunity to send a different message. One message that there is another way to avoid conflicts, based on generosity, tolerance and knowledge of the other, because that was always the way in which the Greeks proceed. We are a people who have spread around the world and who have conquered it with tolerance, with work, with kindness. I think it is fair that today the legitimate representatives of the people of Greece that we sit here should dignify this condition of this people that we represent and have the generosity to make an effort so that we can unanimously recognize the fair claims of the people who accompany us today and of many others who profess different confessions. I hope that we will be able to do this, and I hope that we will be able to convey good news to all these people, to all these Greeks who have come to their home today to see how their representatives were able to represent them properly. Nothing else and thank you very much.

All this I suppose had grown, and much, the number of pockets along Mr. Zapatero's mandates. It's all over with President Rajoy's politics. The whole system has changed, and it has changed with clear ideological motivation: to reduce the number of university students, which is what Minister Wert and Mr Rajoy wanted. Excluding students from families with reduced economic power and, of course, also reducing the budget for scholarships. And all of this was achieved by two decrees that changed the whole system. Decree 1000/2012 of June 29th, which increased academic requirements to get help for the studies, increased them in an absolutely unfair way. I remember him if his lords forgot. Now, in order to have a supplementary scholarship, a scholarship that pays the tuition and that pays in addition a supplement for the residence and help in the studies, the student and the student must have a 6.5. I mean, those who have money go from course to course and get enrolled with 5, those who don't, with 6.5. If this isn't unfair from every point of view, you'll tell me. But besides in 2013 there was another decree, the 609, which checked all the economic allocations of the various aids for university degrees and masters, changed the typologies, removed the aid to mobility and reduced the count to half. First of all, that the increase in academic requirements to access a scholarship left out of the scholarship and study aid system in 2014 and 2015 around 53,000 students, who before had a chance to obtain a scholarship and who now do not have that possibility; 53,000 students out of the system. The number of fellow students has grown to 3% per year when they grew from 30% to 35% per year in previous years.
