ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-04-04-DSPG023.u213	Thank you, Mr. President. Good afternoon, everyone. The management and development of industrial soil and services in Galicia is assumed by two public entities: Xestur, dependent on the Infrastructure and Living Council, and the Atlantic Business Soil Society, participated and managed by the Ministry of Development through Sepes. Most of the polygons are promoted and managed by Xestur, but there is in our country 18 initiative and management of Atlantic business soil. There's evidence of discoordination between the two entities, and we find ourselves with planning situations and prices that have no justification and that produce competitive disadvantages between polygons and others close to them depending on which entity promotes them. It is essential to develop an industrial soil map in Galicia that helps to analyse and plan business policies and implement strategic decisions on the development of new industrial soils and coordination of existing ones, linked to an industrial plan for Galicia that is truly coherent. Irresponsible decisions should never be made again on industrial soil based on electoralism or on donations to local landowners without any sense and who only claimed to bury millions and millions of euros. The way should be to achieve a single management of industrial soil through the society that already manages it in Galicia, as it is Xestur. It has to be an instrument that facilitates industrial expansion throughout Galicia, favouring areas with less growth and with worse access to communication infrastructures. But, as we shall see in the case described below, the situation is totally contrary to these objectives mentioned in the preceding paragraph. We refer to the industrial polygon of Vimianzo — to greet the mayor and the part of the corporation, who accompany us today — promoted by the company Soelo Empresarial del Atlantico, dependent on the Ministry of Development. This polygon was part of the compensation on the Death Coast for the Prestige disaster. Fifteen years later the polygon is not fulfilling its objectives. One of the causes is the lack of direct connection to the highway of the Coast of Death, another commitment by the governments of the state and the Union. On a recent visit to the Infrastructure Advisor's area, he undertook to develop a branch of access to the polygon that would, among other things, prevent the passage through the centre of Vimianzo from heavy traffic coming from the Camarinas area. We hope that as soon as possible the Councillor will carry out this commitment here, at the parliamentary level, with precise deadlines, dates and budgets. Another of the causes - and in this case the main - that the Vimianzo polygon and other similar ones, such as that of Malpica and many others throughout the country, fails to guarantee the objectives of industrial development, in this case in the Vimianzo area, and that it would also have an immediate possible solution if it wanted to be done, is price discrimination with respect to other nearby polygons, such as, in this case, that of Carballo, promoted by Xestur. The plots for industrial use in the Vimianzo polygon have a high price compared to those of Carballo ranging from 40% to 90%. In the case of industrial-third-party mixed-use plots, or directly tertiary, prices in Vimianzo exceed more than 100% those in Carballo. On the other hand, the defects in the construction of the Vimianzo polygon - which seems to be that the chosen place is not the right place - make up extra costs for demos, containment walls, etc., which can reach 40 euros/square metre. This price difference situation has no justification for promotional and market costs. The logic, really, would be the opposite, for having the Carballo polygon was close to a much more populated urban and comarcal concentration and direct access to an infrastructure like the Coruña-Carballo highway. Therefore, the origin of this competitive disadvantage is the different commercial strategy and cost imputation of Atlantic Business Soelo with respect to Xestur. We believe that this Parliament cannot go back to an area like Vimianzo and the whole of Soneira, with enormous potential in its agri-food industry and many others that develop in the area, and resign themselves to seeing all its industries march to other areas because it is impossible to develop competitively in its territory. In the face of all that we expose, we believe that the Government of Galicia has to intervene urgently in order to ensure that at least the prices per square meter of the two polygons are balanced -- we refer to Vimianzo and Carballo -- and that we avoid the competitive disadvantage in this case of Vimianzo. - I'm sorry, I'm wearing a fucking flu and... To this end, we present the following non-legislative proposal: "The Galician Parliament urges the Galician Union to make the necessary managements before the Ministry of Development to instruct the Atlantic Business Land to develop new parcel sales processes in the Vimianzo polygon at high prices that make it competitive and, at least, similar to those in the Carballo Polygon; urges the Ministry of Development, through Atlantic Business Land, to develop initiatives to promote the Vimianzo Polygon, by, among other things, conducting studies of possible industrial and tertiary specialization of it and to promote the possibility of establishing collaborative economic initiatives that make it attractive to the companies of the Costa da Morte; and, finally, to initiate the procedures to achieve the transfer of all industrial land offers in Xestur, avoiding its current dissolutions between its promotions and those undertaken by the Xesto ministry of transfer; An alternative to this last proposal that we could accept as an amendment, if it facilitates its approval by this Parliament, could be the maintenance of property as it is in the Atlantic business society, transferring only management to be able to coordinate it organically with Xestur. We will also accept, as it could not otherwise be, the amendment of the People's Party's Addendum, which extends this recommendation of price bonus to all polygons belonging to the Atlantic business soil throughout the country. We are sure that this logical demand for the industrial development of Vimianzo will be taken into account by this Parliament and we hope that the corresponding management of the Government of the Union to the Ministry of Development will result as soon as possible so that this situation will not be maintained in the coming months. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-04-04-DSPG023.u230	To formulate the proposal, you have the floor to Mrs. Antón.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-04-04-DSPG023.u231	Thank you, President. Ladies and gentlemen, good afternoon. On 23 October 1987 the Council of Europe, in an emotional act with international impact, here, in Santiago de Compostela, declared the Way of Santiago as the first European cultural itinerary. This was a turning point for the Way because, on the one hand, it was the culmination of a process, and on the other hand, I assumed the beginning of a new stage for the route Xacobea and hence for Galicia and the city of Santiago. He said it was the culmination of a process of recognition of the Jacobean route as a collapse of European construction, of its identity, through the common cultural heritage, claimed by researchers of the Jacobean fact, by intellectuals, by politicians all over the world and, as it does not, by Spain as well. Not in vain, the Way of Santiago was the protagonist of one of the major exhibitions that took place in Belgium on the occasion of Europalia 1985. Spain, a guest country, wanted to show the full potential of our country with the cock of its entry into European communities, and the Spanish Government chose the Way of Santiago to appear before Europe. The importance of the Path and the success of this exposure is reflected in an anecdote. Three days after the opening of the exhibition, more than 400 requests for visits by groups and groups were already fixed. While the heart of Europe was recognized on the Way of Santiago, Elías Balinas Sampedro, the parish priest of the Cebreiro, began untiring work, began a journey without going back. From Roncesvales to Santiago marks with yellow arrows the trail of the Way, promoting its cleansing and recovery, and creating a network of collaborators that would derive from the associations of friends of the Way of Santiago, a ribbon that ended and culminated in the Assembly of Jaca, of which it is also now fulfilling thirty years and established the principle of the present pilgrimages. "I am preparing a great invasion," Elijah Balinas replied to the Civil Guard's request when, near a border crossing, they asked him what the meaning of those yellow arrows was. And he wasn't wrong. Civil society, public administrations, supranational institutions, understood that the Way was an example for the somewhat convulsed situation of the end of the 20th century, marked by the overcoming of geopolitical blocks in Europe itself with the fall of the Berlin Wall, and the growing globalization in we have economic and social, with greater mobility as a result of the opening of borders, of advances in the media and installation in the daily life of so-called new technologies. This statement by the Way of Santiago as the first European cultural itinerary was the starting point for the development of a programme launched by the Council of Europe, based on the appreciation of culture and the promotion of cultural tourism with the creation of thematic networks, with the common heritage as the central axis. The Way represents the values that inspired the creation of the Council of Europe itself, as they are with regard to human rights or democracy, but also the foundations of the Institute of Cultural Itineraries: diversity, cultural democracy, mutual understanding and cross-border exchanges; a institute that was created — as you all know — in 1998 as a technical agency to accompany the promoters of the already declared itineraries and to contribute to developing new routes for their certification. To be a European cultural itinerary, in the words of the former director of the institute, Michel Thomas-Penette, is characterized, firstly, by being a great European issue, capable of raising fundamental issues: identity, relationship between cultures, memory, theme from which one or more networks of collaborators give knowledge and organize a set of projects. It's about reaching cultural and tourist products rooted in precise territories, accessible to most and with a discourse of interpretation and mediation that contributes to a better dialogue and better reciprocal understanding of Europeans, bringing together their widely dispersed memories. But an itinerary is something alive, which from knowledge, interpretation and enjoyment of that past must serve to build together the future. That is what happened with the Way following this statement by Santiago by the Council of Europe, which calls on the authorities, institutions and citizens to perform different tasks. For example, the identification of the roads of the European territory; the establishment of a system of signification of the main points of the itinerary; the development of coordinated actions of restoration and heritage value; the launching of cultural animation programmes in order to rediscover this historic heritage; the promotion of exchanges between the different peoples and cities along the Way; and the encouragement, within these exchanges, of contemporary cultural artistic creation and so on. The Council of Europe itself organizes congresses and other activities to promote all these objectives, and creates an image, a corporate identification manual of the Way that we all recognize today. An idealized vision of the yellow-colored old age shell, like the yellow arrow of our friend Elijah Balina, and the blue of the flag of Europe. The Greek Government of the time, understanding the message and recognizing the opportunity it meant, was able to gather this idea and shape it in the administrative and governmental context, creating a management unit for this purpose: the Society of Management of the Jacobean, beginning a plan that met with all and each of the sections of Santiago's declaration and some, such as the creation of the road shelter network, thus recovering one of the strengths of the same: hospitality. A plan that had an objective, the celebration of the Holy Year of 1993, with a renewed vision: the Xacobeo, following the star of the great events held in our country, such as the Barcelona Olympics or the Seville Universal Exhibition. Xacobeo, like these events, has been influenced by the involvement of the Spanish Government, the creation of an inter-administrative body that is still in force today, as is the Xacobeo Council, where they are cited — as you all know — by the autonomous communities through which the Way and the Central Administration speak. The very essence of the Way, the values in which it settles and intends to spread, together with the recurring celebration of the holy years, makes the Way something unique, unique, which was referendumed after the success of 1993, in the holy years of 1999, 2004 and 2010. The Path reaches the recognition of the World Heritage Site in 1993. In 2004 the Prince of Asturias da Concordia was awarded the award, as well as the title of Grand Cultural Itinerary for the Council of Europe itself this same year, who would like to highlight in this way that the Way is the capitalized route a primer inter paris. There's no other route in the world with more recognition. The Way of Santiago was chosen at its own time by the Council of Europe to illustrate European identity, but today it transcends this very reality to become the international symbol of tolerance, as regards diversity, being religious, cultural, political. The Way is the meeting space between men and women around the world, in the search and experience of fundamental humanist values, such as solidarity, freedom, tolerance, sacrifice, generosity. The Way is a reality of social cohesion, where citizens from all over the world relate to each other and to a cultural and natural heritage, resulting in an exchange of personal and collective experiences that fill this path with renewed life. The feedback from the Way itself through the experiences of every day of the pilgrims, whatever their motivation, and the local population, strengthen their reason for being. Today, thirty years later, Santiago's statement remains perfectly vigilant, and we believe that from Galicia, from Santiago de Compostela, the commemoration of this last anniversary is an exceptional occasion to claim the spirit of Santiago's Way for a Europe that lives immersed in a crucial moment to address its future and its role in the world. This anniversary must also be, in our opinion, a moment of reflection on the network of declared cultural itineraries that travel across Europe, for its management, its role, the existing collaborative formulas and how to cope with a shared future. Currently there are 32 declared routes. Spain participates in 22, and Galicia, in addition to the Way, is part of nine cultural itineraries of such varied themes as the Vikings and Normans, the network of Cluniacens sites, the European itineraries of the Jewish heritage or the European route of thermal heritage and thermal cities, among others. To visualize these itineraries, and to give them a boost in their knowledge and recognition, is an opportunity that we should take advantage of, because, ultimately, we are sinking into our own roots, into our own history, and into the history that unites us with other peoples and people. The membership of the large networks of cultural and natural tourism that promote values of cultural democracy, of freedom, of understanding must also be understood as a competitive advantage to highlight itself and differentiate from other cultural products. Being different is enriching. That is why we put forward this non-law proposal, in the understanding that no one doubts that it is an opportunity to revive the idea of a compromised Europe. That's why I want to take advantage of and close this intervention with the same words as it closes and ends Santiago's statement: "that the faith that has encouraged pilgrims throughout history, and that has brought us together in a common aspiration over national differences and interests, inspires us today as well, and particularly youth. going along these paths to build a society based on tolerance, with respect to others, freedom and solidarity." Nothing more and thank you very much.

"We are witnessing an unprecedented situation and in view of the need for the international community to take urgent action and act accordingly. In early 2016, for the first time since World War II, the number of people displaced from their homes as a result of conflicts, persecution and human rights violations in their countries of origin exceeded 65 million, according to UNHCR data. A figure that would increase if we added thousands of people who had been forced to travel through natural disasters or poverty. This situation reveals the inability of the international community to resolve and prevent conflicts and situations of humanitarian emergency, as well as the lack of political will to comply with international human rights obligations and international protection. Last September 22 was a year since the leaders of the European Union agreed on an emergency relocation mechanism to share the responsibility of those seeking asylum. However, tens of thousands of people are still trapped in refugee camps in deplorable conditions. In the case of Spain, only 6% of the relocation commitments were met. At this rate, it would take eight of us to meet these commitments. The figures speak for themselves. More than a year after these commitments, Spain has hosted 1,141 people, a figure far away from the 17,337 people that the Government has committed to welcoming. In view of the above, the Galician Parliament publicly declares the urgent need for the governments of the European Union, and in particular that of Spain, to significantly speed up the process of transferring refugees to Spain through their relocation and resettlement, and to comply with the commitments agreed with the European Union to host 17,337 refugees within the time frame."

ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-04-04-DSPG023.u257	Thank you, President. Good afternoon, ladies. The Outpatient Emergency Service of the Autonomous Community of Galicia, since its inception, has had the main objective of ensuring urgent health care of the highest quality to the entire population of Galicia. The current configuration is a result of adaptation to the population's epidemiological and demographic needs, the characteristics of the Welsh health structure and technological advances. Its structure includes a coordination centre and an urgent health transport network. The central, unique for all Galicia, is a medical regulatory centre, which means that all health demands of the population are met by health personnel and all existing resources are geared towards identifying the care needs and giving the most appropriate response to the clinical situation that the patient presents as soon as possible. It is, of course, a vertebrate element of all health care in the Greek community. It's a power plant equipped with the technology needed for the immediate detection of demand increases, multiple-victim incidents, epidemiological alerts, etc., which enables you to strengthen, in a short period of time, work shifts, both health and non-health personnel. Since its inception in 1995, the call-in route, both of users and of health resources, has been common, having no impact on the system over the twenty-one years of activity. In any event, in the pursuit of further improvement of the system, in 2016 a call router program was implemented which, together with Tetra network — the corporate network of digital communications of emergency and security of Galicia — allows the entry of some ambulances to be provided directly by the optional responsible for the mobilization without the intervention of the teleoperator. It is being studied whether this possibility is beneficial for the whole network. At present, all existing fleet ambulances in Galicia have just been renewed, counting on the latest technological and medical advances necessary for the most advanced care for patients, which constitute the fundamental axis of our work. It can be said, without a doubt, that the resources of the health transport in Galicia are among the most modern and advanced in Europe. This is the service most appreciated by the Greeks among all who offer the Galician Health Service to citizens. The satisfaction surveys, which take place every two years, achieve elements of high levels of satisfaction on the part of users. Last year, in 2015, 98.9 % of users would use the service again in case of need, and the average service score is 9.1 on a scale from 0 to 10. Unquestionably, this is a model coveted by the medical emergency services of the rest of the country, since, still with high dispersion, a complex meteorology, many kilometres of roads, and a high population ageing, guarantees all Greek citizens the accessibility, equity and efficiency of high-capacity assistance. The current contracts, signed between the Galician Public Health Urgency Foundation-061 and the adjudicatory companies of the Land Urgent Health Transport Service, fulfil their obligation with respect to the staff employed, to comply with the applicable provisions on labour law enforcement and to the provisions of the collective agreement of the sector throughout the entire performance of the contract. As labour and social legislation establishes, lawyers, with regard to their staff, will maintain all the rights and obligations inherent in their status as employers, without the right to claim by themselves any right in relation to the Public Health Emergency Foundation of Galicia 061 or the Health Council. This means that if the workers or their works councils appreciate any irregularities in the fulfilment of the convention or of the legislation in force, they must address the competent bodies to ensure workers' rights in the field of safety and health at work, since this breach, accredited by a firm resolution of the competent authority, could be sanctioned by the foundation, which in other cases would not have authority. The shift of staff at the focal point, both sanitary and non-sanitary, 7 hours at daytime and 10 hours at nighttime, is strictly consistent with the current legislation. The Public Emergency Health Foundation of Galicia-061 meets regularly with the adjudicators of the earth's urgent health transport services, with the telephone operation service, as well as with the staff meeting of the foundation itself, with bi-monthly periodicity, and whenever a request is made, in an extraordinary way. At these meetings, these are all those proposed topics that may relate to the quality of the service and to improvements in its provision, as well as the follow-up to the contract. Even so, the Health Council has met with representatives of the different trade union organizations of the sector whenever they demand it. But we must not forget that we are talking about private business workers who have just won the public contests that the Health Council called. It's the adjudicate companies that must meet with the corresponding representatives of the workers, analyze their demands and negotiate collective agreements in each sector. The task of the Administration is to assess the strict performance of contracts, and it should not be involved in the negotiations of workers and their employers, as they have repeatedly expressed both employers and trade union organisations, and as established in the current labour legislation. Support ambulances have been used since the start of the first emergency health transport network in Galicia, i.e. since 1998. We're talking about both basic life support care ambulances, as well as staff staffing for 061, as well as conventional ambulances. The use of resources other than the emergency transportation network is always valued by the optional staff of the coordination centre, and this is one of the great benefits of having a "medical regulation" rather than the "resource clearance" type as in other autonomous communities. The services used as the only means of response are scarce, and they are used in patients who do not have a vital emergency. It should be noted that since 2010 the use of this type of resource has declined significantly, and in 2016 it has decreased to 3.88%. Since 2003, which has expanded with two new advanced life support ambulances in the cities of Coruña and Vigo, the network has not changed until 2010, when the new medical base of the Navy was inaugurated. Recently, on July 1, 2016, it just expanded with 5 new basic life-support care ambulances, in Coruña, Tomineño, Rianxo, Ourense and Vilagarcia, and with an increase in its schedule in other 5: Baiona, Marín, and 3 in Vigo city. This expansion is the result of the continuous improvement of 061, which continuously analyzes, among others, demographic changes, changes in the demand for care, changes in the road network, coverage of health centres and average response times, with the aim of adapting the network to the new needs of patients. The Urgent Health Transport Network managed by 061 is currently composed of 2 medicalized helicopters, 11 advanced life-support ambulances and 106 basic life-support ambulances, with the number of vehicles currently higher than those contemplated in the prayers. Thank you very much. We'll continue on the second exhibition.

The process of recruiting the earth's urgent health transport service, carried out by the public foundation recruitment body Emergency Sanitary Offices of Galicia 061, was carried out in accordance with the Consolidated Text of the Public Sector Contracts Act and the one established in the nail of particular administrative clauses, ensuring at all times strict compliance with the applicable legislation. To date, resolutions on 11 appeals before the Madrid Central Administrative Court of Contractual Resources have been in favour of the foundation —11 appeals submitted —. Even so, before the Dispute-Administrative Chamber of the Supreme Court of Justice of Galicia, three appeals were filed: a resolution favourably for the foundation, February 2, 2017; and the other two, pending resolution. In 2016, in relation to compliance with the quality indicators of the services collected in the prayers, where the data collection, the state and maintenance of the vehicles are valued, the quality felt by the users, the training of staff and the follow-up of the operating protocols of the service, the average percentage reached by the tax companies throughout the community was 94%. The state of health transport in the Autonomous Community of Galicia has, as has already been pointed out, been among the most modern and advanced in the state. It is a fully renewed fleet, technologically advanced and of high comfort to the patient during his assistance and transfer. Obviously, there is no need to solve a situation that is modelled in the Spanish state and systematically observed and imitated by the rest of the health services because of its effectiveness and the guarantees it offers to users. So, for example, the implementation of our programs by health services from other autonomous communities, such as the "School Alert" program in the Balearic Islands; or the fact that we are once again pioneers at national level in implementing a strategy aimed at cardioprotecting all citizens, regardless of their proximity to the most advanced health resources, through the "RCP in the classroom" program. These merits are recognised as being awarded the European 400+ Quality Management Foundation, as well as multiple awards from its special programs, such as the Leave, Progaliam or Anxioedema Alert Program. Recently, the Spanish Society of Cardiology and the Medical Journal collect these programs as the best health ideas in Spain. This doesn't mean that the Health Council doesn't continue as it has up to now, analyzing the sector and adapting to new needs, centres and technological advances. It is very remarkable that the urgent health transport network had not changed during the years 2005-2009, when it ruled its party in the Bipartite. However, in 2010 a new ambulance, as I said, was incorporated into the Navy, and in 2016, five new basic life support ambulances. Look, we've made it clear that this is a great service. And, of course, a lot of this is due to the workers of 061, who do a great job. And our function is to ensure the legality of contracts and, of course, that both labour conditions and services are met to the Greek users, which logically is the main end of the Galician Health Service. We will continue to fight for improvement, for modification, for enlargement, according to the needs of the people of Greece. And in that, of course, we will surely be united with you too. But, of course, we're still fighting for better health care. Thank you very much.
